### 2026-09-04T21:00:00+03:00 · INT-320 · doing
- Goal: make Vizite presentable as an embeddable SERVICE — a partner drops a "Start pre-visit" button into its own app, the branded intake runs for that partner's patient, the note flows back. This is an ARCHITECTURE change, so ADR first (ADR-029) + a minimal, secure, fail-closed scaffold for ONE dev-tier synthetic partner tenant. Not a production build.
- THE SURFACE IS A SIGNED TICKET, NOT AN EMBEDDED UI, AND THAT IS THE DECISION EVERYTHING FOLLOWS FROM (ADR-029). An embedded Vizite UI in the partner's origin would put a PHI surface in a third-party origin and make the partner page an injection surface. Instead: the partner BACKEND mints a launch ticket server-to-server (`POST /embed/v1/launch-tickets`), the patient device presents it (`POST /embed/v1/launch`), and only then does the branded intake start. HS256 over the stdlib `hmac` primitive `room_tokens` already uses — no token library — closed claim set `{tenant_id, nonce, iat, exp≤120s, purpose}`, no clinical or personal value in the token.
- ISOLATION IS IN THE SIGNATURE, NOT A PARAMETER (inv. 37). The tenant is bound inside the signed payload and the signing key is that tenant's own (from OpenBao). A ticket minted for tenant A cannot verify under tenant B's key, and the verifier additionally checks the claimed tenant against the tenant whose key verified it — there is no free-text tenant field to forge. The nonce is single-use and consumed LAST, only after signature, structure, purpose, clock and tenant all pass, so a tampered replay fails the signature long before `consume` and can never burn a genuine nonce (a property with its own test).
- BRANDING IS CONSTRAINED DATA, NOT MARKUP, SO THE EMBED IS NOT AN INJECTION SURFACE. `infra/tenants/embed_registry.yaml` is a second list (the ADR-026 shape) addressed by `tenant_id`; the loader refuses anything but six-digit hex colours, a bounded display name and id-shaped asset/consent refs — URLs, schemes and markup are rejected at load. `device_functions` may not be enabled for any embed tenant (ADR-020). The file ships with ZERO prod rows and one synthetic `dev-partner-alpha`; a `prod` row is refused without recorded `dpa_ref` + `brand_permission_ref`.
- NO NEW SERVICE, NO NEW TABLE, NO NEW PERSONAL-DATA FIELD. The routes live in services/api (already exposing /healthz /readyz /metrics /version — inv. 15 satisfied) under a new SEVENTH consent-scan marker `partner_embed` caged to `/embed` (the door has no Vizite data subject; consent is collected by Vizite's own docs/legal flow afterwards, inv. 4/18). A partner-supplied patient identifier / note-back correlation is deliberately OUT of scope — linking it is a new personal-data category and recipient needing human sign-off, so the ticket carries no patient id and nothing is persisted or linked (inv. 17/18). The note flows back only through the existing connector path (ADR-026): the embed adds no new outbound PHI path.
- THE PARTNER BUTTON HOLDS NO SECRET. `packages/embed-sdk` (`@intake/embed-sdk`, TS strict) is a thin launcher: on a tap it calls the PARTNER'S own backend for the launch response and only ever opens an `https` Vizite URL — a non-https partner endpoint or launch URL is refused, so a tampered response cannot become an open-redirect or a `javascript:` sink.
- Tests added (with near-miss negatives): `tests/embed/` — `test_tickets.py` (round-trip, cross-tenant refusal, tamper/expiry/skew/purpose/ttl/replay, tampered-replay-does-not-burn-nonce, closed claim set), `test_registry.py` (constrained branding, device_functions-off, zero-prod-rows, unknown-tenant fail-closed, shipped file dev-only), `test_launch.py`, `test_router.py` (admin-only mint, unknown-tenant 404, no-key 503, unconfigured 503, replay/tamper/malformed id-shaped 400, marker-present + scan-green, audit entries), `test_service.py` (OpenBao resolver fail-closed, build helper, launch branches). `packages/embed-sdk/src/index.test.ts` (14). `intake_platform.embed` and `intake_api.embed` at 100 % branch coverage.
- Result: `tests/embed/` 89 passed, 100 % branch on all new modules; ruff + `ruff format --check` clean; `mypy --strict` clean on the new + changed files; the INT-038 consent route-scan (`tests/legal/test_consent_guard.py`) 30 passed with the new marker; backlog + smoke 28 passed at 243 entries (242→243 with a stated reason, the three count pins bumped, files/11 re-rendered); metrics catalogue + SLO 39 passed (two `planned` embed signals); threat-model parity 18 passed; `check_sec_row_linkage.py` OK (INT-320 → embed); TS embed-sdk typecheck/lint/test green. `make check` fan-out recorded in `docs/evidence/INT-320.md`.
- Invariants touched and still passing: 4/18 (consent renders only from docs/legal, fail-closed; no text authored here), 6 (refusals + audit are id-shaped codes; no token/key/PHI), 14 (tenant bound in the signature; signing key OpenBao-only; branding constrained; note-back via connector only), 15 (no new service; new metrics catalogued), 17 (human-gated content left empty, fail-closed), and ADR-020 (`device_functions` cannot be enabled for an embed tenant).
- Decisions / ADRs / `00_STATE.md` rows: **ADR-029 (Accepted)** — the embeddable white-label handshake; files/10 §9 gains row `embed` with its threat-model section; INT-320 filed in the backlog. Re-litigates none of D1–D15 or ADR-001…028; builds within ADR-020/025/026.
- Blocked on (human + one sentence; docs/backlog/blockers.md INT-320, no HB id minted — coordinator to allocate where none exists): (a) embed consent texts under the tenant's dev legal root → HB-L27; (b) real partner onboarding — executed DPA, brand-use permission, OpenBao signing-key custody/rotation and a shared (Redis) nonce store for multi-replica single-use → no HB row yet; (c) a partner patient-id / note-back correlation as a new personal-data category + recipient → no HB row yet. Each mechanism ships wired and fail-closed; none of the content is invented.
- Next: on sign-off of (b), the first real `prod` embed tenant row + its OpenBao key seeding + the shared nonce store; the consent-texts authoring rides INT-210.

### 2026-09-04T00:20:00+03:00 · INT-203 · awaiting-human (`blocked:runtime`)
- Goal: acceptance verbatim — *"WAF blocks OWASP CRS suite; CrowdSec scenarios on; certs automated"*, against a title naming five things: Traefik, Coraza CRS, CrowdSec, cert-manager and the prod egress proxy. files/10 §4's `Edge:` bullet is the spec and it already records the defect not to reintroduce — the allow-list that did not cover server-side fetches the console itself provokes.
- THE ACCEPTANCE WAS MEASURED GREEN AND IS NOT CLAIMED, WHICH IS THE WHOLE STORY OF THIS ITERATION. On 3 Sep the edge booted on this host and the corpus was decided against the **real** engine — Traefik 3.3.7 + Coraza + OWASP CRS 4.0.0, cold `compose up` to green in 28 s, seven CRS families refused and five ordinary Turkish clinical turns served. Then the owner removed Docker from the laptop entirely and the GPU server turned out to be a pod with no runtime of its own. The clause is a property of a **running** tier, so it stops being a gate; the run is recorded with its date and hashes in `docs/evidence/INT-203.md` §3–§5 and the issue stays open. The move I did not make is the one invariant 17 names: asserting `SecRuleEngine On` and `@owasp_crs/*.conf` from the policy file and ticking the clause. **That assertion is satisfied by a WAF that 403s every request**, including all five Turkish clinical turns, which takes the product off the air while the test stays green.
- THE WAF IS A PHI CHANNEL, AND IT TOOK BOOTING IT TO SEE. A real captured line: `[data "Matched Data: sUEv, found within ARGS:json.text_tr: Göğsümde şiddetli ağrı var' UNION SELECT …"]`. CRS rules carry `logdata`, so every match quotes the matched argument back, and on this API that argument is the reason a person is seeing a doctor; `[uri …]` carries the query string. Shipping it to the SIEM trades an injection *attempt* for a disclosure that **succeeds**, is durable, and looks exactly like a control working. The answer is not redaction — a filter that misses once has leaked, over attacker-chosen text — but `EdgeEvent`, a closed record with no field a sentence could occupy, the discipline `TransferRecord` already applies to the egress register. Five keys read, `data`/`msg`/`uri`/`hostname`/`client` never looked up, family from the CRS *file name* which is a constant of the ruleset, unknown lines dropped rather than shipped. Traefik's access log is `defaultMode: drop` and the CrowdSec parser extracts two values, neither a path.
- THREE SCENARIOS IN ONE FILE LOAD AS ONE. The renderer first emitted all three as documents of a single `scenarios/intake.yaml` — valid YAML, valid CrowdSec, and `cscli scenarios list` showed **one**. CrowdSec's local inventory keys on the file, so two were silently absent: a tier reporting "scenarios on" while running a third of them, which every config-file test would have passed. One file per scenario now, plus `orphan_scenarios()` for the delete direction a write-only renderer never notices, plus a count assertion in both directions.
- THE REBINDING CASE WAS ALREADY GREEN BEFORE I TOUCHED IT, AND SAYING SO IS THE POINT. Sixteen of eighteen egress assertions passed on the red run: INT-018/160/161 built this properly, and `EgressPolicy.authorize()` already resolves per request and connects to the pinned address, so there is no cached answer to poison. Two genuinely failed. (a) The **proxy tier's** rebinding window: squid evaluates `dst` against an IP cache with a six-hour positive TTL, and the in-process guard cannot cover it because behind a proxy the process deliberately does not resolve at all — `ipcache_size 0` plus one-second TTLs makes the ACL check and the connect share one lookup. (b) The **fourth shape** of ADR-025's bypass, which is not a call site but a *configuration*: a personal-data transport pointed at the forward proxy, the one arrangement in which **neither** point resolves. Refused at construction. Near-miss control beside it: a package pull may still use the proxy and the hostname must still survive to the CONNECT line.
- Tests added: `tests/infra/test_waf_rules.py` (the named test, 39 — policy schema, renders, the log boundary, CrowdSec and certificate clauses), `tests/infra/test_edge_egress.py` (18 — the prod proxy), `tests/infra/test_edge_stack_integration.py` (17, `integration` — the booted corpus), `tests/fixtures/waf/corpus.yaml` (12 cases, both verdicts) and `tests/fixtures/waf/coraza-blocks.log` (real captured lines, provenance in the header). The integration suite **fails rather than skips** without the pinned artefact: 17 errors, 0 skipped, rc=1, and it never reaches `docker compose`.
- Result (`make check` / `make safety` / `make security`): **`make check` rc=0** (5527 passed, coverage 95.21%, every floor met, plus lint/format/mypy --strict/pnpm lint+typecheck+test/check-i18n) and **`make safety` rc=0** (1271 + 121 passed, triage branch coverage 100%, the nightly red-team plan consulting no judge). **`make security` has NO exit code to report and none was invented.** It did not exist on this branch's base — INT-019 merged to main after this worktree was cut — so `origin/main` was merged in first (two conflicts, both additive narrative, resolved keeping both sides; `files/11` regenerated by the repair script; the marker sweep over every tracked file found none). The run that then started was killed part-way by the owner's second constraint: gate 1 of 12, gitleaks over all 703 commits, finished green with no leaks; gate 2, semgrep, was mid-scan; gates 3-12 never ran. `bash scripts/ci/no_placeholders.sh` rc=0. `make check-integration` was NOT run: its first line is `docker compose up`, which the constraint forbids, and local Postgres and Redis are stopped — environmental, reported rather than worked around. All exit codes captured with `; echo "rc=$?"` on the command itself, never through a pipe.
- Invariants touched and still passing: **6** (the edge log boundary above; Traefik field selection; the CrowdSec parser; `test_the_transfer_record_of_a_denial_carries_no_path_or_query`); **13** (two registry rows with probes and licences, both `self_hosted_now: false` on the EHRbase precedent — built, verified, deployed nowhere; no new metric name, because the catalogue is closed to files/08 §4 and `sec.waf_blocks_total`/`sec.cert_expiry_days` already exist as `planned` and are Traefik's to emit); **14** (three raw LICENSE blobs read and hashed — Traefik MIT, the Coraza middleware Apache-2.0 from inside the artefact, the CRS Apache-2.0 at its own pinned commit; the CrowdSec blob's commit cross-checks against the running container's build string); **17** (nothing mocked, nothing skipped, the blocker filed); **19** (every component open-weights-equivalent and licence-verified from the blob, never a badge).
- Decisions / ADRs / `00_STATE.md` rows: **no ADR number minted.** One is arguably owed and the case is in the evidence — the edge introduces a new boundary component and a new shared control surface (`intake_platform.edge`), and §6.2's refusal of the proxy for personal-data transports narrows an existing documented composition. If the coordinator wants it recorded, the case is written and needs only a number.
- Blocked on (human + one sentence + HUMAN_BLOCKERS id): **two asks, no id minted.** (1) *Where may the edge tier run — a cluster, a runtime on a machine we may install one on, or is the acceptance descoped?* Explicitly **not** HB-I1/HB-I2: those are disk and RAM on a machine that may now run nothing regardless, and the tier is ~200 MB and booted in 28 s, so this is permission and siting rather than capacity. (2) *Which fully-qualified names does the edge serve, and which mailbox owns the ACME account?* `domains: []` and `INTAKE_ACME_EMAIL` unset; the renderer emits the issuer and **no** `Certificate`, because an empty `dnsNames` is refused by cert-manager in a cluster, later. Row and narrative in `docs/backlog/blockers.md`.
- Next: INT-204 (mesh mTLS + default-deny NetworkPolicies) shares the runtime blocker and should not be started before it is answered. The egress half of this issue stands alone and is worth taking regardless.

### 2026-09-03T23:55:00+03:00 · INT-314 · done
- Goal: acceptance verbatim — (1) *"`confirm_tr`, `action_tr`, `do_nots_tr` and `ask_one_tr` become per-locale maps on the same schema as the five INT-297 migrated, with `tr` required, so invariant 10 holds for the WHOLE of a red-flag script rather than for its question half"*; (2) *"the byte-identity discipline is repeated, not assumed: the pre- and post-migration trees compile to identical served bundles for `tr`, authored string AND `expand_tr` expansion, using the same comparator INT-297 committed — a refactor that changes one Turkish RED sentence is the most dangerous edit in this repository"*; (3) *"the `selectable_locales` clamp is REMOVED by this issue rather than left dormant, and a test asserts a pack carrying all four in a second locale is now selectable — a clamp that outlives its reason is a silent restriction nobody can see"*; (4) *"invariant 21 is untouched: RED stays route x register x location with no permitted default, and nothing here lets a locale change which action resolves for a location"*.
- Plan: extend INT-297's committed machinery rather than write a second migration style — `SPOKEN_FIELDS` beside `MIGRATED_FIELDS` in `protocols.migrate.locale_maps`, `authored_baseline(..., fields=)` in `tr_baseline`, a second committed baseline taken from the pre-migration tree, then the DSL, the clamp, the four consumers and the YAML.
- THE CLAMP IS DELETED, NOT DEFANGED, AND THAT NEEDED A SECOND LOCALE TO PROVE. `pack_locale.MONOLINGUAL_SPOKEN_FIELDS`, `_is_monolingual_spoken` and the `common &= {DEFAULT_LOCALE}` line are gone; the four fields are simply in the intersection now. With one locale registered a clamp to that one locale is **invisible**, so `test_a_pack_carrying_all_four_fields_in_a_second_locale_is_now_selectable` widens `KNOWN_LOCALES` to `{tr, de}` under `monkeypatch` for its own duration and asserts `selectable_locales(pack) == {tr, de}`. Its near-miss removes the `de` entry from **each** of the four in turn and gets `{tr}` every time: removing the clamp did not remove the rule it stood in for, it made the rule apply to those four fields instead of standing in front of them. No language is offered — HB-O5 and HB-C19 are untouched.
- THE ONE ASYMMETRY IS `do_nots_tr`, AND CONFLATING ITS TWO EMPTIES WOULD HAVE BROKEN TURKISH. Most RED branches carry no do-not list; the DSL spelled that as an empty tuple. An empty **map** keeps that meaning and constrains no locale, so `pack_locale` passes it as `None`; `{tr: []}` is an author saying "no do-nots in Turkish" and does constrain. Reading an empty map as "speaks no locales" would have made every rule without a do-not list unselectable in every language, Turkish included. New `LocaleList` type: `tr` required and equal arity **when entries exist**, empty map permitted — and the arity rule is stricter in spirit than `choices_tr`'s, because each line of a do-not list is a separate instruction, not a rephrasing.
- BYTE-IDENTITY, STATED AS COMPARATOR OUTPUT: **13 documents, 58 fields, 68 authored + 68 `expand_tr` strings, 0 differing, VERDICT IDENTICAL** — and INT-297's own baseline re-run beside it, 96 documents / 468 fields / 485+485 strings, also 0. The comparator is INT-297's `authored_baseline`, generalised over a field set rather than replaced, against a baseline generated **before** the schema moved. Three of the 68 expansions are refusals (`"Hemen **112**'yi arayın."` and two siblings) and the refusal string itself is compared, so an outcome that moved would be a diff even where the bytes did not. INT-297's served-bundle test is **unmodified** and still passes, against a baseline taken before *either* migration.
- INVARIANT 21 SURVIVED BECAUSE ONE FIELD IS TEXT **AND** ROUTING. `primary`, `secondary`, `show_112`, `numbers`, `notify_staff_immediate`, `carry_child`, `on_yes`, `on_no`, `on_no_answer` all stay single-valued — `test_a_locale_may_not_change_which_action_resolves_for_a_location` passed at RED, which is exactly its point. But `ask_one_tr`'s answer picks ON_SITE or REMOTE, so files/04 §8.2's one-question gate now runs over **every** entry in the map (`unknown_question_refusals`). Its limit is written down rather than implied: `_question_count`'s mark count is language-neutral, its comma-joined-interrogative half is Turkish morphology. Running it per locale widens the first and takes nothing from Turkish; naming another language's detector belongs with registering that language.
- Tests added (with near-miss negatives): `tests/protocols/test_locale_maps.py` +12 (four-field map shape one assertion per field; dropped-`tr`; unregistered `de`; **registered** `de` with one fewer do-not so the refusal comes from arity and not registration; empty map accepted; clamp-name absent; second locale selectable; four-way removal; per-locale routing refusal; per-locale one-question gate) and `tests/protocols/test_migration_is_identity_for_tr.py` +4 (spoken byte-identity; baseline self-coverage — a comparator over an empty set passes vacuously; RED rows against INT-297's pre-migration bundle; no unmigrated spoken field left, over two trees INT-297's sweep did not reach). Fixtures reshaped in 12 further test modules.
- TWO TESTS WERE WRONG AND COVERAGE, NOT A FAILURE, FOUND BOTH. The do-not arity test passed for the wrong reason — `de` was unregistered, so the refusal came from the easier rule and the arity check was never reached. And `LocaleList`'s empty-map branch was unreachable because pydantic does not validate a `default_factory`; an explicit `do_nots_tr: {}` case now covers it. Both fixed, both recorded in the evidence rather than quietly corrected.

### 2026-09-04T00:55:00+03:00 · INT-315 · doing
- Goal: acceptance verbatim — *"the workspace side is resolved too: a directory that git can see inside the repository must be refused as a restore target even when it is reached through a symlink whose `Path.resolve()` lands elsewhere - today only the target is resolved, so the comparison is between a real path and a symbolic one and it silently answers "outside""*; *"the case that produced it is the regression test: a symlinked `.cache` directory inside the worktree, which is exactly what this repository's own fresh-worktree deid-model workflow creates, so the fixture is the real workflow rather than an invented one"*; *"the near-miss control passes unchanged: a genuinely external target is still accepted, and a relative path that escapes upward is still refused - a fix that refuses everything would pass the first clause and destroy the drill"*; *"no restore ever writes patient data inside the repository, which is what this guard exists for and what the symlink quietly permitted"*.
- BOTH VARIABLES CARRIED `.resolve()`, AND THE COMPARISON WAS STILL ASYMMETRIC. The old guard read `resolved = Path(target).resolve()` and `root = Path(repo_root).resolve()`, which *looks* symmetric and is not: `resolve()` answers where the bytes land, and workspace membership is a question about the path. When a checkout's own child is a link out — `.cache` symlinked to the model store — `(REPO / ".cache" / "restore").resolve()` lands in the store and the containment test answered "outside", while `git status` answered "inside". A restored cluster there is one `git add -A` from being committed and one `make check` from being scanned, which is the invariant-3 property the guard exists to hold.
- THE FIX READS BOTH SIDES BOTH WAYS, AND BOTH HALVES ARE LOAD-BEARING. `_readings()` returns `os.path.abspath` (git's reading: collapses `..` textually, follows no link) and `Path.resolve()` (where the bytes land), deduplicated when they agree. Containment under any pairing refuses, so accepting requires every reading to agree the target is outside and an ambiguous spelling fails closed. The workspace root is read both ways for the same reason — either side can be the symbolic half, and a checkout named `/tmp/wt` with a target spelled `/private/tmp/wt/.cache/restore` is caught by the lexical target against the resolved root and by no other pairing. Where no link is involved `abspath == resolve`, `_readings` returns one element, and the check reduces to the original single comparison: no behaviour change for any path that has none.
- THE CONTROL RUN CAME BEFORE THE FIX, WHICH IS THE ONLY WAY CLAUSE 3 MEANS ANYTHING. A guard that refused every target would satisfy clauses 1, 2 and 4 and destroy the drill, which has to restore somewhere. The guard body was replaced with an unconditional `raise` and the class re-run: both near-miss controls failed (rc=1, 4 failed / 7 passed), so they are gates and not sentences. The variant was discarded and the file restored from a copy — never `git checkout <file>`.
- Tests added (with the near-miss negative for any safety rule): six in `tests/infra/test_restore_drill.py::TestDrillPhiSafety`, plus the `_symlinked_cache_worktree` fixture that builds the real fresh-worktree deid-model shape in `tmp_path` rather than depending on one machine's `.cache`. Three refusals — `test_a_symlinked_cache_inside_the_worktree_is_still_inside` (asserts the *old* reading explicitly, `target.resolve() == model_store / "restore"`, before asserting the refusal, so it states what it defends against), `test_any_symlinked_directory_inside_the_workspace_is_refused` (the rule is about links, not about the name `.cache`), `test_the_workspace_side_is_resolved_too` (link on the root, real path on the target, target's parent a link out — no single-sided reading answers it). Two **near-miss controls** — a genuinely external target is accepted, including the model store the workspace links to, a sibling whose name merely *starts with* the workspace's name (a string-prefix "fix" would refuse it), and `/restore` itself; and an upward escape that genuinely leaves is accepted while the pre-existing one that comes back inside is still refused, so `..` is read as a path and not as punctuation. ### 2026-09-03T23:40:00+03:00 · INT-183 · done
- Goal: acceptance verbatim — *"registry rendered; benchmark trigger; red cell blocks release visibly"*, for the files/08 §148 *Models & self-hosting registry* module. `apps/admin-web` did not exist; this issue creates it as the second TS workspace package, holding one module built **inside** the INT-180 shell rather than beside it — both routes are `AdminRoute` descriptors, so they inherit that issue's structural properties whole (a route cannot exist without an audit action, the audited-route list stays derived from FastAPI's own table, the mutating route resolves to a registered `DangerousAction`).
- THE MODULE IS A PROJECTION, NOT A SECOND MATRIX, AND THAT IS THE DECISION EVERYTHING ELSE FOLLOWS FROM. `GET /admin/models` reads `observability/registry.yaml` through `intake_probes` — the same package that runs the 60-second sweep and evaluates the release gate — so `intake-probes` became a declared dependency of `intake-api` for exactly the reason INT-241 declared `intake-metrics`. There is no console-side list of components to fall out of date, because there is no console-side list. `test_rows_are_exactly_the_registry_components` fails in both directions.
- "RED" IS THE INT-022 GATE'S VERDICT AND NOTHING ELSE, AND THE NEGATIVE CONTROL IS WHAT MAKES THAT MEAN ANYTHING. A cell is `BLOCKING` **iff** `evaluate_release_gate` names its component; `test_the_decision_is_the_int_022_gate_not_a_second_implementation` asserts the two lists are equal element for element, so a divergence is a failure rather than a drift. files/08 §6's rule has two arms — *"`self_hostable=false` or no healthy fallback"* — and the first is honoured by being **unreachable**: `RegistryEntry` already refuses a `phi_path` row that is not `self_hostable`, so that state cannot be written down. Everything else worth saying is amber and **does not block**: a licence the registry itself records as unread, a PHI-path row declared but not running, a probe with no result in this sweep. `tests/fixtures/registry/amber_only.yaml` is a registry whose worst state is exactly that, and the release proceeds over it. Without that control an implementation blocking on *any* imperfection would satisfy the clause and teach a reader nothing from the colour.
- THE BLOCK IS LOAD-BEARING, NOT DECORATIVE. `releaseControl.enabled` is `false` while any cell is blocking, the tile lists each blocking component with the gate's own sentence, and `renderModelsModule` **throws** `InconsistentReleaseState` when handed a payload claiming an unblocked release over a blocking cell. A stale tab, a cached body or a tampered response cannot buy a green console — a block is only "visible" if it cannot be rendered away.
- THE BENCHMARK TRIGGER RUNS ITS LATENCY LANE AND REFUSES ITS QUALITY LANE, AND THE REFUSAL IS THE DELIVERED STATE. The latency lane executes the row's own probe through `run_probes_once` — the same sweep the runner performs, narrowed to one component by handing it a client map of one — so a benchmark and a probe can never disagree about what "healthy" or "over budget" means. Clients are injected everywhere: the suite opens no socket and the route opens none unless `INTAKE_PROBE_CLIENTS=dev`; unset, it returns its plan with `measurement: null` and says why, because a benchmark that reached nothing reports that and never a zero. The **quality** lane refuses with `no_named_corpus`: `BENCHMARK_CORPORA` ships **EMPTY** — the same fail-closed shape INT-180 gave its `ip_allowlist` and tenant `deployments` — because **HB-C10** and **HB-A13** are open, and a corpus id invented here to light the lane up would be the invented value invariant 17 forbids.
- Tests added (with the near-miss negative for any safety rule): `apps/admin-web/e2e/models.spec.ts` (13, the named test) and `tests/admin/test_models_module.py` (25), plus `tests/fixtures/registry/amber_only.yaml`. Near-miss controls and what missing them would cost: **amber must not block** → "red blocks the release" would be satisfied by blocking on everything; **`test_amber_fixture_is_genuinely_amber_and_not_merely_unprobed`** guards the control itself, because an all-green fixture would make the near-miss pass vacuously; **a payload claiming an unblocked release over a blocking cell must throw** → a stale response would paint a green console over a red gate; **a `Measurement` with no corpus must not construct, on either side of the wire** → an unlabelled figure is a claim.
- THE FIXTURE IS A RECORDING, NOT A MOCK. `apps/admin-web/e2e/fixtures/models.payload.json` is what the API serves, emitted by `make admin-fixtures`, and `test_committed_fixture_matches_the_served_payload` holds the committed file against the live response. A hand-written mock would have been the second, hand-kept copy invariant 13 forbids — it would keep passing on the day the registry changed and the console stopped matching it.
- ONE RED RUN CHANGED THE DESIGN. With the projection built, `test_models_route_refuses_a_role_outside_the_set` failed: the read had been left at `_ANY_CONSOLE_ROLE`, which handed the platform's self-hosting posture to `support` — a role scoped to `ONE_PATIENT_GRANT`. - Result (`make check` / `make safety` / `make security` summary): exit codes recorded in `docs/evidence/INT-183.md`, captured directly with `; echo "rc=$?"` and never through a pipe. `services/probes/src` joins the strict-mypy fan-out (it already passed; now it cannot stop). One catalogued metric added, `admin.model_benchmark_requested_total`, with `docs/runbooks/model-benchmark.md` — `DangerousAction.__post_init__` refuses an entry without either.
- Invariants touched and still passing: **6** (the trigger's body is a closed two-key shape and an unexpected field is refused by *name*, never echoed by value; the metric's three labels are a staff subject and two closed enums); **13** (the console renders what the registry declares, and the release gate is called rather than copied); **17** (`BENCHMARK_CORPORA` empty, `pin: null` where no pin exists, `measurement: null` where nothing ran — three absences left absent); **19** (`model_id@revision` rendered where a row carries one, and the unverified licence surfaced as a standing finding rather than smoothed over); **20** (`Measurement` cannot be constructed or rendered without a named corpus and a date, in Python and in TypeScript, so there is no second path from a number to the page); **22** (quantity and unit cross the boundary as one atom with `useGrouping: false`).

### 2026-09-03T21:30:00+03:00 · INT-180 · done
- Goal: acceptance verbatim — *"roles per 08 §2; break-glass with reason+approver; role-matrix contract tests for /admin/*"*, against a title naming four properties: Keycloak RBAC/ABAC, audit on every action, IP allow-list, 4-eyes primitives.
- THE AUDITED-ROUTE LIST IS DERIVED IN THREE LAYERS, AND THE SECOND ONE ALMOST SHIPPED HOLLOW. (1) `ADMIN_ROUTES` is a descriptor tuple carrying method, path, `audit_action`, `permitted_roles` and — for mutating routes — `dangerous_action_id`; `intake_api/admin/routes.py` declares **no** route with a decorator, it walks that tuple and registers each descriptor through one wrapper, so the audit action is structurally attached rather than remembered. (2) The tests read the list off the running app, never from a list. (3) Every route is then **driven** and the chain inspected: exactly two entries, `<action>.intent` then `<action>.success|failure`, sharing one content-addressed `resource_ref`, ascending `seq` — refusals included, because a refused admin request is the one an investigator wants. **The near-miss that mattered was my own:** the first derivation read `app.routes` flat and returned `set()`, because FastAPI 0.141 keeps an included router as a `_IncludedRouter` holding `original_router`. Every gate reading it would have passed by accident. The fix walks the tree, cross-checks against the served OpenAPI document, and **asserts non-emptiness** — a derivation that can return nothing is not a gate.
- NINE TESTS FROM NINE ISSUES CAUGHT THE CONSOLE ARRIVING UNDECLARED, WHICH IS THE SYSTEM WORKING. The first `make check` came back rc=2 with twelve failures, nine of them one defect: `tests/legal/test_consent_guard.py`, `test_dsar_flows.py`, `test_profiles_companions.py`, `tests/clinician/test_doctor_link.py`, `tests/api/test_consent_routes.py`, `test_notification_prefs.py`, `test_taxonomy_feed.py`, `tests/experience/test_csat_flow.py` and `test_wait_metrics.py` all independently noticed six new `/admin` routes declaring nothing to the INT-038 route-table scan. The fix is **not** an exemption entry: `consent_guard.py` gains a seventh caged marker, `administrative_action`, accepted only under `ADMIN_PREFIX = "/admin"`, with the same cage rule and a near-miss negative control (`/v1/borrowed` and a `/administration/x` lookalike both still report as unguarded). Why a marker and not a scope: a console action has a **staff** principal and no data subject at the door, and files/06 §2 enumerates no staff-access purpose, so naming one would assert a lawful basis this repository does not have. It is attached at the **same** `add_api_route` call that attaches the audit action, so a route cannot land undeclared to either scan.
- WHAT FOUR-EYES COVERS, AND THE LIMIT WRITTEN AS A PASSING TEST. Covers, each a typed refusal: a different account (`four_eyes_self_approval`, which increments the intrusion signal `admin.four_eyes_self_approval_attempt_total`), a different WebAuthn credential, a different browser session, an eligible approver role, a different **organisation at tier 3 only**, a fresh single-use assertion, an approval window that closes, one decision per action. A shared **AAGUID is deliberately not** a refusal — it identifies an authenticator *model*, so checking it would refuse two people holding the same brand of key. **Does not cover: two accounts held by one person.** Nothing in an OIDC claim set says two subs are two humans; `test_two_accounts_held_by_one_person_are_accepted` asserts the acceptance so the gap is a passing test rather than a silence, and closing it needs identity proofing or an approver pool the actor cannot enrol into — an operating control, not another field. Break-glass is registered **tier 2, not tier 3**: §2.5 asks for a distinct human, authenticator and session, which is exactly the tier-2 ceremony, so a break-glass approver **may be a colleague in the same organisation**, and that is stated rather than implied. `ApprovalBrief` carries the requester's full identity block, tier, target, reason class, the **full justification free text**, the controller-instruction reference, the evidence, both timestamps and a rendered `approver_rule` — an approval screen showing less is theatre.
- THE CONSOLE CANNOT REACH THE CHAIN'S HEAD, AND A TEST SAYS SO. An AST walk over every source file in `intake_platform/admin/**` and `intake_api/admin/**` finds no mention of `AuditChain`, `AuditStore`, `build_worm_bundle`, `export_worm`, `verify_entries`, `verify_worm_export`, `run_daily_verifier` or the attribute `head`; `AdminAuditSink`'s public surface is exactly `{"record"}`; no `/admin` response carries a 64-hex string; and the head demonstrably advances while none of it is echoed. **The blocker is HB-I12, not HB-I8** — HB-I8 is the pgBackRest retention window; HB-I12 is *"name where the audit chain's head is published, outside the `platform_admin` blast radius"*, and it exists because a chain detects an attacker who edits a row and stops, not one who recomputes forward. The console sits inside that radius: it can append and nothing else.
- Tests added: `tests/admin/` — `test_rbac_matrix.py` (14, the named test), `test_admin_audit.py` (16), `test_four_eyes.py` (23), `test_oidc_verifier.py` (24), plus `admin_harness.py` and a recorded Keycloak claim fixture. Near-miss controls and what refusing them would cost: **the marker outside `/admin`** → a `/v1` data route dodging a consent scope; **a shared AAGUID accepted** → two colleagues with the same key model unable to approve anything; **`alg: none`, HS256-against-a-public-key and RS512** → the whole JWS confusion family; **an expired/foreign-issuer/wrong-audience token**; **a JWKS with no usable key** → every token failing with an unknown-`kid` code that hides the real fault.
- TOKEN VERIFICATION IS HAND-WRITTEN, FOLLOWING THIS REPO'S OWN PRECEDENT, NOT A NEW DEPENDENCY. `intake_api.dpop` already verifies a compact JWS over `cryptography` with **one** accepted header and no algorithm negotiation; `intake_platform/admin/oidc.py` does the same for RS256, so the alg-confusion family has no code path rather than a guard on one. The JWKS is read from a **mirrored file**, never fetched: the most privileged surface in the system must not answer 503 because Keycloak is briefly unreachable, key rotation should be a deploy with a diff, and CI must never dial a live service. All twelve §2.1 roles are declared in `infra/keycloak/dev-realm.json` — configuration, not code — with **no user added and no client secret anywhere**.
- Result (`make check` / `make safety` / `make security` summary): see the run recorded below; exit codes captured directly with `; echo "rc=$?"`, never through a pipe. Five group-O metrics moved `planned` → `live` in `observability/metrics_catalog.yaml` after the catalogue gate correctly reported them as *"catalogued as planned but actually emitted"*. `docs/runbooks/break-glass.md` is the runbook every one of their alerts links to, and `DangerousAction.__post_init__` refuses an entry without one.

- THE BASELINE WAS TAKEN BEFORE THE FIRST LINE OF SCHEMA CHANGED, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE METHOD. A refactor across 116 YAML files cannot be reviewed by reading it. So the first artefact built was `protocols.migrate.tr_baseline`, which reads the **raw** YAML and takes the `tr` entry out of *either* shape — plain string or locale map — and records the authored string beside its `expand_tr` expansion, the bytes that actually reach TTS. Run on `origin/main` it produced the committed baseline; run on the migrated tree it must produce the same bytes. One module, two trees, and the diff is the only thing that can tell a rename from a content edit.
- Plan: baseline first (pre-migration, committed as a fixture), then the failing tests, then the schema, then the script, then the tree. Recorded red at **2026-09-03T05:31:20Z**, rc=2, `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'protocols.locale_text'` on both new modules.
- THE RESULT IS STRONGER THAN THE ACCEPTANCE ASKS FOR. **96 documents, 468 authored fields, authored and expanded, byte-identical.** In the served bundle the ONLY difference anywhere is one identifier: `openehr_bindings.json` and the 13 clip rows' `subject`/`text_tr`/`scripted`/`text_hash` are byte-identical outright, and `questionnaire.json`, `slots.py` and `slots.ts` are byte-identical apart from the embedded `resolved_protocol_hash` (`1ff5ba02…` → `fa1ba233…`), which moved because the resolved document's shape moved. The test **substitutes the hash back rather than excluding those three artefacts** — dropping them to avoid one changed line would drop the FHIR item texts and the slot schemas with them, which is what the comparison exists for. The second difference, the clip id gaining the locale, is asserted rather than tolerated: a test pins both the old and the new shape, so a deliberate re-keying cannot be confused with an accidental content edit.
- THE MIGRATION IS A LINE EDIT, NOT A RE-DUMP. `yaml.safe_dump` reflows every quote, comment and block scalar and would bury five real changes in three thousand cosmetic ones. `migrate_text` rewrites only the five fields' own lines and re-indents their continuations (block scalars `>-`, wrapped plain scalars, flow and block sequences all handled), so `git diff` shows exactly the fields that moved. `migrate_file` refuses to write unless the parsed result of the line edit **equals the declared shape-level transformation**, so an edit that lost a byte fails loudly instead of landing. Idempotent (`--check` exits 0 on the migrated tree) and reversible (`unmigrate_document` is the exact inverse for a one-key map and refuses a wider one rather than choosing a locale to drop). Multi-document and unparseable files are passed over rather than rewritten blind — a pack is always one document.
- Tests added (with the near-miss negative for any safety rule): `tests/protocols/test_locale_maps.py` (**16**) and `tests/protocols/test_migration_is_identity_for_tr.py` (**7**); tests/protocols 469 → **492**. Over a mapping, `tuple()` yields the **keys**: no exception, no failed validation, and `("tr",)` satisfies `tuple[str, ...]` perfectly. In typed mode a patient would see one answer chip labelled `tr` where three should be — and the same field feeds `free_numeric_critical_path_steps`, so invariant 22's "no free numeric text entry on the clinical critical path" gate would have passed a pack that cannot be completed without one. Both now project through `entry_for(..., protocol.locale)`, and the silent one is a named function whose docstring records why it may not be inlined. Written up in full in the evidence.

### 2026-09-03T07:05:00+03:00 · INT-312 · done
- Goal: acceptance verbatim — *"the redactors see what speech actually produces: digit groups (a spaced or hyphenated eleven-digit run), Turkish digit words, and the mixtures a recogniser emits mid-utterance - with the committed SPOKEN_TCKN_FORMS corpus as the input rather than a normalised copy of it"*, *"the checksum stays the discriminator, so this remains a TCKN redactor and does not become a number shredder: a checksum-FAILING eleven-digit accession number still survives untouched, which is INT-102's existing near-miss control and must keep passing unchanged"*, *"the normalisation happens where it can see the whole utterance and cannot reach the note: it is a redaction concern, not an ASR feature, and nothing added here may put identifier handling on the speech critical path (invariant 9) or retain audio (invariant 3)"*, *"the two controls that hold the line today are not weakened: deidentify_for_egress still fails closed ENTIRELY without the NER tier, and LlmSpan still has no field a transcript could occupy - both asserted before and after"*, *"any leak-rate figure lives only in docs/evidence/ with a date and the named corpus, described as a measurement of that corpus on that day (invariant 20)"*.
- THE CHECKSUM IS NOT AN OPTIMISATION, IT IS THE ONLY THING STANDING BETWEEN A REDACTOR AND A NUMBER SHREDDER. Turkish digit words are ordinary clinical speech: *"On beş gündür ağrım var"* is fifteen days of pain, and over-masking it is a **clinical harm rather than a nuisance**, because a redacted duration is a lost history. So the fix assembles candidate digit sequences and lets the published checksum decide, and the near-miss control is not a near-miss in the string sense — `SPOKEN_ACCESSION_FORMS` is the *same four shapes* over the same checksum-failing accession number, built by the same factory, so the masked case and the surviving case are exactly one field apart.
- Plan: failing tests first against the committed corpus (recorded red: 7 failed / 31 passed in `test_adversarial_rt12_14.py`, plus an ImportError in the new platform module), then the smallest implementation that satisfies them, then the boundary assertions that keep it where it belongs.
- THE CANDIDATE WINDOW IS TOKEN-ALIGNED, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE ANSWER TO "WHY CAN IT NOT SWALLOW A NEIGHBOUR". A *chain* is a maximal run of digit-bearing tokens — ASCII digits, or one of the ten Turkish digit words — separated by **exactly one** space, non-breaking space or hyphen. A candidate begins at the first digit some token contributed and ends at the last digit some token contributed, so it can never begin or end inside a token: a thirteen-digit barcode offers **no** eleven-digit window at all, `2026-08-30-12-34-56` offers none either, and `Oda 4 100 000 000 78` yields the identifier without the `4`. The scan is left to right and resumes at the token AFTER a match, so two identifiers in one utterance are both masked and the words between them survive. One separator character and only three of them: a looser rule reaches across `0,5 - 5,5 mIU/L` and across a thousands separator. `on`/`yirmi`/`yüz` are deliberately not digit words. Case handling is canonical spelling plus sentence-initial capitalisation rather than `re.IGNORECASE`, which would drag in Turkish dotted/dotless folding where `I` is both the capital of `ı` and the ASCII capital of `i` — an ambiguity with no right answer and no place in a redactor.
- Tests added (with the near-miss negative for any safety rule): `tests/platform/test_spoken_identifiers.py` (**new, 31**) and the rewritten `TestRT13SpokenIdentifier` in `tests/safety/test_adversarial_rt12_14.py` — the adversarial suite 110 → **121**, tests/safety 519 → **530**. The corpus grew from two shapes to four (`spaced-groups` and `digit-words` unchanged and still asserted present by id, so a fix cannot quietly narrow the corpus to what it handles; `hyphenated-groups` and `mixed-words-and-groups` added), all built from `SYNTHETIC_TCKN` and consumed as-is. Near-miss controls, and what refusing them would cost: **the same four shapes with a failing checksum** → a lab accession number said aloud, whose destruction takes the provenance of a result with it; **a spoken duration** → *"On beş gündür ağrım var, iki üç gece de uyuyamadım."*, untouched; **a duration touching the identifier** → `… yedi sekiz beş gündür`, identifier masked and `beş gündür ağrım var` intact; **a thirteen-digit barcode**, **an ISO timestamp run**, **a comma/dot/double-space/spaced-hyphen/slash between groups**, **a digit run glued to a word**, **a spoken TR mobile** (leading zero, refused by the checksum, owned by the phone pattern), and **a 4-grouped IBAN** that still comes out as one `[IBAN]`. INT-102's `test_the_near_miss_control_a_checksum_failing_number_survives` is **byte-identical** and passed in both the red and the green run.
- Result (`make check` / `make safety` / `make security` summary): `make check` **rc=0** — ruff, `ruff format --check` (747 files), `mypy --strict` over all eight roots (329 files), 5022 passed / 49 deselected at 95.52 % against the 85 % floor · schemas 134 · protocols+safety 999 at 99.05 % · triage 530 at **100.00 %** branch · voice-clips 836 at 100 % · voice-runtime 1222 at 98.60 % · metrics 197 · learning 144 · apps/mobile 1617 across 83 files · check-i18n 30. `make safety` **rc=0** — 18/18 mutations detected, the adversarial suite 121, `nightly_red_team.py --plan` invoking nothing. `redaction.py` is **82 statements / 20 branches at 100 %**. An earlier `make check` failed rc=2 on the generated `files/11_ISSUE_BACKLOG.md` being stale after the status flip; `scripts/backlog/render_backlog.py` regenerated it (done 114 → 115, todo 112 → 111). `make security` not run — INT-019's gate set, and this change adds no dependency, image, endpoint or secret. `origin/main` then moved (INT-138, #458: 239 → 240 entries plus new mobile records screens), so `rebase_backlog.py` merged and repaired in one pass, the one unmerged path — the GENERATED `files/11_ISSUE_BACKLOG.md` — was re-rendered from the merged YAML, `tests/backlog` + `tests/smoke` were green (28) before the merge was committed, a sweep of every tracked file for the three conflict-marker forms found nothing, and both gates were re-run on the merged tree: `make check` **rc=0** (5030 passed, apps/mobile 1753 across 86 files) and `make safety` **rc=0**. Nothing called a model, a vendor or a network; no test sleeps.

### 2026-09-03T06:05:00+03:00 · INT-102 · todo (six of seven acceptance clauses met; the seventh is a defect for the coordinator)
- Goal: acceptance verbatim — *"injection by speech and hidden-PDF text, triage manipulation, proxy impersonation, spoken TCKN masking, vendor failover — all pass; nightly job budgeted"*, plus files/04 §6's *"required in `make safety`"*. RT-09…RT-14 are all blueprint-defined on files/04 line 206; every transcription in the suite is a verbatim substring of that line, re-read at test time, with a paraphrase control the same check rejects.
- A SUITE OF SIX ANECDOTES WOULD TEST SIX STRINGS. What this defends is invariant 14 — *speech, documents and records are data, never instructions* — and a suite whose payloads all read "ignore previous instructions" proves that a filter matching that phrase works. So the cases are the boring ones: a patient QUOTING their doctor's instruction, a symptom description containing a tool name and a JSON fragment, a document whose header is an imperative, Turkish imperative mood that is grammatically ordinary, and content aiming at the model's OUTPUT rather than its behaviour. Each text family is asserted as an **indistinguishability**: the attack and a benign twin one field away are treated identically and only the digest moves, which is what "data, never instructions" has to mean if it is to survive a phrasing nobody thought of.
- Plan: three test modules written first against support modules that did not exist (recorded red), then the corpus + scenario table, then the nightly job, then the Makefile wiring (a second recorded red — four build-graph assertions failing against the real Makefile). The near-miss control for each family, and what refusing it would cost: **quoted instruction** → a quoted dosing sentence, because reported speech is how patients give medication history; **tool-shaped text** → a quoted `"SYS 180 / DIA 110"`, because a quoted machine reading is often the only blood pressure a patient can give; **ordinary imperative** → the RED scripts themselves, read off `files/protocols/base.life_threat.yaml` rather than retyped (`Hemen **112**'yi arayın.` · `Ağzını veya boğazını açıp bakmayın.` · `Çocuğunuzu kucağınızda … tutun.`), zero findings on the dynamic path and verbatim on the scripted one, because invariant 21 makes the bare imperative plural the CORRECT RED register and a guard reading "imperative" as "injection" deletes the RED path; **forged fence** → a barcode label that is sentinel-SHAPED and harmless, since the nonce is derived from the text it fences; **imperative header** → the identical sentence rendered VISIBLY (so the split measures visibility, not a string) and a real Turkish lab footer, *"Sonuçlarınızı hekiminize gösteriniz."*; **emit-not-obey** → the same rule set still reaching GREEN and still firing RED without the demand, plus an in-schema completion that still parses; **undeclared speaker** → an acknowledged proxy, a genuine self-session and the assisted case; **identifier in speech** → a checksum-FAILING accession number that survives untouched, which is what makes this a TCKN redactor rather than a number shredder; **degraded path** → a healthy adapter still routing, a self-hosted endpoint still accepted, and the vendor families still allowed on the DEV tier.
- THE NIGHTLY JOB IS KEPT OUT OF THE GATES STRUCTURALLY, NOT BY CONVENTION. Four mechanisms, each with a control proving the assertion can fail. (1) The script imports **no transport** — a test walks its own `ast` for import roots including function-scope ones, and the control is the same walk over `extraction/gateway.py`, which finds one. The parser skips comment lines without ending a recipe, because GNU make does — a parser that stopped at a comment would stop seeing exactly the lines an invocation would hide behind. On this tree `--run` exits 3 without caps, 3 over the ceiling and 4 with a valid budget, having invoked nothing: `client_from_env` returns `None` and says why, and reports **no synthesised score** (invariant 20 — a red-team number nobody measured is worse than none, because no diff makes it look wrong).

Both pattern tiers — `intake_platform.deid._TCKN` and `redaction._TCKN_CANDIDATE` — are `\b[1-9]\d{10}\b`, a CONTIGUOUS eleven-digit run, and an identifier said aloud arrives from an ASR as digit groups (`100 000 000 78`) or as Turkish digit words. Neither is masked. A test that pre-normalised its own input would be a green suite over an open hole. It is not a leak on this tree, and the two controls that hold the line are asserted instead: `deidentify_for_egress` fails closed ENTIRELY without the NER tier (so the spoken form does not reach a cloud call scrubbed — it does not reach one), and `LlmSpan` has no field a transcript could occupy, every string field being ref-shaped. The residual is a fully configured dev tier, which would scrub names and pass the spoken identifier through.
- Next: **one finding for the coordinator to allocate, no id minted** — a digit-run normalisation for spoken identifiers, spanning `packages/intake_platform` (`deid.py` + `redaction.py`, which owns its own leak-rate measurement in `tests/security/test_deid_leak_rate.py`) and an ASR-side normalisation in the voice runtime. Two packages and two acceptance surfaces, so a second issue rather than a second half of this PR. The reproduction is committed: `SPOKEN_TCKN_FORMS`, `SYNTHETIC_TCKN` and `utterance_with` in `tests/safety/adversarial_corpus.py`. A second, smaller finding is recorded on the scenario rather than fixed: files/04 line 206 says *"PolicyGuard logs `injection_suspected`"* and it does not — `ViolationClass` has no such member and never had one. The signal is real and belongs to the DATA BOUNDARY; adding the member to satisfy the sentence would put injection handling on the speech path, which is the one place it must not be.

This repo records a visit DATE and no time of day (the `visit.date` slot), so the exact rule cannot be evaluated by anything here. The anchor is the START of the visit day in Türkiye: never later than the appointment, so never later than the spec's value — it errs strictly towards LESS exposure — while still leaving ≥ 48 h after any appointment on that date. - Decisions / ADRs / 00_STATE.md rows: a refused local match does NOT count toward the speech-failure counter or the cooldown, and nothing wires it to. `unclear` counts what the CONVERSATION could not place (remedy: ask again, once); `voiceFailures` counts what the AGENT could not say (remedy: a bounded ladder retry). Conflating them fails both ways — a patient who mumbles would mute the agent and put a false caption on screen, and a dead TTS lane would start abandoning questions the agent heard perfectly to the typed steps. INT-294's own asymmetry is the argument: `speech_skipped` moves the cooldown and not the caption because a turn nobody attempted says nothing about the lane, and a refused utterance says nothing about it either. A re-ask, by contrast, IS an ordinary speaking turn and takes the cooldown like any other. ### 2026-09-03T02:40:00+03:00 · INT-293 · done

- Goal: the fix above inverted clinical meaning and must not merge as it stood. Two independent adversarial reviewers found the same defect from different examples — Turkish negation is POST-POSED, and a matcher that reads whole words in sequence turns "Ateş yok" ("I have no fever") into `["fever"]` and "Terleme var, bulantı yok" into a flat `["sweating","nausea","none"]`. `DialogueIntake` pre-ticks the candidate and `confirmRow` commits it in ONE tap, so a patient who denied a symptom handed their doctor a note asserting it. A dropped answer is visibly missing; an inverted one looks like data.
- Plan: refuse rather than guess. Build a conservative OVER-refusing detector, never a Turkish negation parser (INT-248 already recorded what happens when the morphology is guessed at), demonstrate the red run against the committed `turkish_negation_adversarial_set` in files/04 §8.2, then restore `>` at the `extract_settled` sequence guard so a refusal costs a round trip instead of locking the extractor out of the same utterance.
- Tests added (with the near-miss negative for any safety rule): apps/mobile/src/lib/intake/answerMatch.test.ts +10 (both reviewer inversions verbatim; the verbal negative across its tense forms; `değil`/`hayır`; and every phrase of `turkish_negation_adversarial_set` run against EVERY option-bearing authored question) plus the negative controls that keep the guard honest — a bare `Yok` is still the `none` option, `Hayır` is still `no`, `Belirli bir düzeni yok` still binds `no_pattern`, and the whole-vocabulary round-trip in both fold directions is untouched and still green, which is what proves the detector never fires on an authored label; apps/mobile/src/lib/dialogue/stream.test.ts +2 (a denied symptom never reaches `pending`, and the extractor is not locked out of the utterance the local read already saw — flip the operator back and it fails).
- Result: `make check` **MAKE_CHECK_EXIT=0** (apps/mobile 1386/1386 across 72 files, tests/release 24/24, all seven pytest coverage runs at or above their floors); `make safety` exit 0 (triage 389/389 at 100 % branch, 18/18 mutations detected, voice-runtime 1136/1136 at 98.71 %). Both on the tree with `origin/main` merged (backlog repaired to 229 entries, pins recomputed, files/11 regenerated). `scripts/ci/no_placeholders.sh` OK after staging. Red run, green run and the refusal/acceptance table are in docs/evidence/INT-293.md.
- Invariants touched and still passing: 1 (the guard only ever REMOVES a candidate — it cannot add one, and a candidate is still a suggestion a human confirms, never a triage input); 2 and 5 (no patient-facing copy changed and no new string, so no `tr`/`en` pair was needed); 17 (no placeholder, no skipped or xfail test — the two committed phrases that bind nothing today are asserted, not excused); 8 (no floor lowered, and no existing assertion weakened to make the change pass).
- Decisions / ADRs / 00_STATE.md rows: the detector is deliberately not a parser. Lexical negatives go by folded prefix (`yok`/`değil`/`hayır`); the verbal negative fires ONLY where a TAM morpheme follows the `-ma-/-me-`, the same discipline INT-248 put on `koy(?!m)`, and a bare token-final `-ma/-me` is never tested at all because that is the deverbal noun suffix and `Kanama`, `Terleme` and `Şişme` are option labels. A negation marker INSIDE a matched label is not evidence against it — it IS the label. `>=` went back to `>` at the sequence guard: the local read is a floor, not an authority, and on every sentence it refuses the extractor's reading is the only reading there is. Also folded in: `confirmRow` gated its note write on the question resolving but confirmed unconditionally, so a confirm could clear the row while the note kept nothing.
- Blocked on (human + one sentence + HUMAN_BLOCKERS id): nothing new. Not claimed: the simulator was not re-driven, and the over-refusals are real — "Ağrı kesici almadım" and the ablative "kanamadan" both refuse the whole read and cost a round trip. That is the trade.
- Next: INT-294 (the TTS admission walk and the mute latch) has not merged yet, so its `voice_doctor.py` rewrite does not exist to defer to; INT-295 (the clipped mode bar) is still todo from the same walkthrough.

- Goal: INT-293 §3 — in Detaylı the mode row rendered "Hastanedeyim" + "Detaylı mod açık" + "Sıranız geldi" as one line that neither wrapped nor scrolled, with the checkpoint chip pinned to the far end by `marginLeft: "auto"`, so the chip walked off the right edge. "Sıranız geldi" is the control for "I have been called in, give me my note now" and Detaylı is the in-hospital mode, so the one affordance built for the situation the mode exists for was unreachable in it.
- Plan: reproduce by MEASUREMENT before touching anything; extract the row into its own module so a test can read its declarations; write the reachability test; fix structurally; then the two other things the acceptance names — the progress counter, and the class swept tree-wide.
- Tests added (with the near-miss negative): apps/mobile/e2e/modeBar.spec.ts (13) + apps/mobile/e2e/fontMetrics.ts (a TrueType advance reader over the shipped `Inter_400Regular.ttf`), and two new sections in apps/mobile/e2e/overflow.spec.ts (5). The negative control re-measures the row EXACTLY as photographed in the smallest role the type scale has (`micro`) and still loses `intake.queue.called` off the 362 pt row — so shrinking type is proved not to satisfy the test. Three mutations of the finished fix were applied and all three were caught (drop `flexWrap` → red on the en/375/1.5 case; never stack → red on the form check; caption back in the row → red on the one-line-of-chrome check).
- Result: `make check` exit **0** · `make safety` exit **0** (triage 100 % branch, 18/18 mutations detected, voice-runtime 98.92 % vs floor 90) · `no_placeholders` exit **0**. apps/mobile 1236/1236 across 67 files.
- THE FILED PREMISE WAS WRONG AND THE CORRECTION CAME FIRST. INT-293 said "at the SYSTEM DEFAULT Dynamic Type (`UICTContentSizeCategoryL`)". The OS category was L, but INT-260's control REPLACES the OS value rather than composing with it, so the two can disagree — and the capture settles it: the `Hastanedeyim` pill measures 195.67 pt wide and 44.0 pt tall in that PNG, which is caption at 26 pt, i.e. the app's LARGEST step. At an OS-following 1.0 the row does fit, with 49 pt to spare. It stops fitting at **1.20** on the same 402 pt phone — iOS's XL. 1.20 is not one of the app's own rungs (`APP_STEPS = [0.9, 1.0, 1.3, 1.6, 2.0]`); it is the continuous scale at which the row first overflows, so the first step a patient can actually select that breaks it is **1.3**, the third of the five — so the defect is reachable without touching an accessibility setting and the issue stands; only the number attached to it was wrong. Acceptance is written against the measurement, because an acceptance line asserting a false premise forces either a false test or an abandoned issue. Corroborated from a screen with nothing to do with this issue: the same simulator at 01:33 today, on a sibling's `(auth)/phone`, renders its CTA pill at exactly **80.0 pt**, which is `spacing.md * 2 + lineHeight("body", 2.0)` and is 56 at scale 1.0 — the device's stored appearance step is the largest one, it is shared by every branch under the same Expo Go bundle id, and nothing on screen says so. The premise was not carelessness.
- THE TEST IS ARITHMETIC, NOT A SHAPE CHECK. `fontMetrics.ts` reads `head`/`hhea`/`hmtx` and a format-12 cmap out of the .ttf the root layout loads and sums the real advances for the real Turkish labels; the arrangement (does the row wrap, does it stack) is read out of the component, so the model cannot describe a layout the app does not have. Calibration on the one screenshot available: model 196.0 pt against 195.67 pt measured — 0.33 pt. Kerning (GPOS) is ignored, so every figure is an upper bound, which is the safe direction for a fit assertion.
- WRAPPING IS WHAT CARRIES REACHABILITY; MOVING THE CAPTION OUT IS WHAT CARRIES THE DESIGN. The mutation run showed the caption could stay in a wrapping row and every control would still be on screen — it would just be three lines of chrome above a question INT-293 already filed as below the fold. The two claims are held by two separate assertions so neither borrows the other's evidence.
- THE SCANNER FOUND TWO ROWS WHERE THE TREE HOLDS FIVE. The first tree-wide gate used a lazy `\n  },` pattern that swallowed every single-line style rule following a multi-line one, and passed with three offenders in front of it. It counts braces now and asserts a floor on how many rows it can see: a gate that matches nothing passes for the wrong reason.
- Invariants touched and still passing: 2 and 5 (no string added, no copy changed — `check-i18n` 24/24, both findings lists untouched); 17 (no placeholder, no skipped test, the new allow-list ships EMPTY); 20 (every figure here is a layout measurement with a date and a named corpus, in docs/evidence only). 1, 3, 6, 9, 10, 21, 22 untouched by construction — this is a layout change with no clinical, audio or numeric-render surface.
- Decisions / ADRs / 00_STATE.md rows: none re-litigated. Recorded in docs/design/mobile-ui.md so a seventh design pass cannot repeat it: the OS Dynamic Type category is NOT evidence of the scale the app renders at, and a row of chrome wraps. The chips now reach the 44 pt floor by BOX rather than by vertical `hitSlop`, because a row that can stack them four points apart is the case lib/a11y/target.ts already refuses horizontally.

## 2026-08-29 — INT-037 merged (PR #311)
- KVKK data-subject rights: export (FHIR + PDF), erasure by destroying wrapped DEKs with the 30-day grace and cancelling live shares, rectification with hash-chained audit, and the m.13 SLA tracker carrying ids only.
- The CI failure was the branch's own test and the implementation was wrong, not the assertion: events.session_id is a FK with no ON DELETE clause, so erasing a subject's sessions violated the constraint on Postgres while SQLite silently allowed it — which is how it reached a green first commit. Erasure now de-links telemetry (session_id → NULL) rather than orphaning or sweeping it: telemetry has its own clock and is PHI-free by construction, so the row survives with the pointer to the erased session cut.
- Nine further defects from a mid-flight review, three of them serious: object bytes were removed INSIDE the row transaction, so one storage failure rolled back the shred while earlier objects were already gone (now a pass before the transaction opens, matching INT-036); an unencodable character made a patient permanently un-exportable — a 500 on every future GET against a duty to produce the document; and every erasure would have registered as an m.13 breach because the grace window and the response window are both 30 days, so a scheduled delete went overdue the moment its grace ended. Also six DELETEs missing tenant_id, a downgrade dropping the parent before its child, and FastAPI's default 422 echoing patient free text into logs (invariant 6, repo-wide).
- Fails closed where the law's text does not exist: export refuses while docs/legal/strings.yaml is unauthored, BUT the request row is created and its clock runs, so an unanswerable obligation stays visible. The PDF writer raises rather than transliterate a Turkish character.
- Recorded, not hidden: erasure writes no DEST-2017 destruction record yet, and execute_due_shreds has no production caller (no services/workers tree), so it is reachable from tests only. ## 2026-08-29 — INT-254 (PR #329), open and green, NOT merged

- The four schema gaps INT-041 found between INT-040's `RedFlagRule` and `files/protocols/base.life_threat.yaml`, plus the real defect found in the same pass. The reproduction goes from **21 validation errors to 13**, and the residue is pinned open by a test rather than absorbed.
- `tense_anchor` was the one with a plausible wrong answer. It is a slot REFERENCE, not a bare key, and the proof is in the code: the `when` tokenizer takes dotted idents, `triage/engine.py` unions the anchor into `referenced_slots(when)`, and `TriageContext.answers` is documented as keyed "by the dotted slot reference exactly as the when predicate spells it". The anchor and the predicate were one namespace at runtime and two in the type. One `SLOT_REF_PATTERN` in `protocols/types.py` now feeds both the DSL constraint and the tokenizer's `ident` group, with a test asserting the tokenizer really uses it. Bare anchors stay valid, so base.intake, all 79 specialty packs and every fixture are untouched.

It also defaulted `status` to `active`. Both are gone; the goldens now run on the document's own spellings.
- `status` stays REQUIRED with no default and the two sourced exemplars carry `status: active`. A default makes a FORGOTTEN status read active — the unsafe direction, and ~30 rules are still to be pasted against this file. A test asserts `is_required()` so the shortcut cannot be taken quietly later.

INT-041 had routed around it by ordering; ordering holds only for callers that happen to run the compiler's check first.

Precisely: gitleaks and semgrep DO run, but as pre-commit hooks rather than in ci.yml, so they gate the laptop and not the merge; bandit, trivy, osv, checkov, license, sbom, phi-scan and claims-scan run nowhere at all. ## 2026-08-29 — INT-230 merged: the research pipeline, named pseudonymisation
- THE ISSUE SAYS "anonymisation (KSVY-16)" AND THAT IS NOT WHAT THIS BUILDS. m.16 was read rather than assumed: 16(1) permits scientific work on data *anonim hâle getirilen* by the controller (KVKK m.28/1(b)); 16(2) is a separate limb under m.28/1(c). A keyed HMAC pseudonym is re-derivable by whoever holds the key, so it is personal data and 16(1)'s route is unavailable. The artefact is `PseudonymisedExtract`, no public name in the package contains "anonym" (a test asserts it), and the provenance sentence a methods section would copy ends "Pseudonymised, NOT anonymised". The pipeline rides 06 §75's other route — consented + ethics id. Also verified: RG 33096 (03.12.2025) does NOT touch m.16 (its thirteen maddes reach articles 1, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10-repealed, 11, 13, 21), so the 2019 wording stands.
- "Validated on corpus" is delivered as an EXCLUSION rule and said so. The sharp half: every bare INT-015 corpus identifier — TCKN, mobile, IBAN, email, date — is *id-shaped*, so a shape rule alone would have admitted every one of them. The pattern tier adds refusals; acceptance comes only from a declared closed vocabulary, which is what stops the case neither catches — "Ahmet" is ASCII, short, id-shaped and carries no pattern hit, and it is refused because it is not a declared member.
- Consent through the ONE evaluator (`consent_state`, the same function `ensure_consent` calls), asked once per patient rather than once per row so the cohort counts people. A refuser is DROPPED, and the test serialises extract + register + the export's audit entries and fails on the id or on any of refus/withdraw/declin/opt_out/excluded — a count of refusals is itself the disclosure. Care consent is not research consent: a patient holding core+voice+documents+learning is not in the export.
- Floor imported, never redefined (a source grep for `MIN_COHORT =`, `k_floor =` and `= 10` is a test). `materialise` reused for the quasi-identifier cross-tab, so one thin cell refuses the WHOLE extract; empty and thin refuse with a string-identical message. No order statistic may even be declared. Both new series are counters, never histograms.

HB-R1 (new): the KAEK decision id, dates, protocol version and recipient list — and it says in the row that `2025-KAEK-51` is the COMMITTEE's own TİTCK registration, not an approval for our study, because that is the plausible wrong answer the repo makes available. HB-L21 (new): which limb of m.16 a consented pseudonymised export rides, if any. HB-I8/HB-L1 cited. `study_registry.yaml` ships `studies: []`, so every export refuses today, and the retention row refuses independently.
- A REAL HOLE FOUND AND CLOSED: pydantic v2's ValidationError text carries `input_value=<the refused value>`, so a registry paste error would have put prose into a traceback verbatim — invariant 6 defeated through the wrapper rather than the validator. `hide_input_in_errors=True`, pinned by a Turkish-diacritic test that failed first.

## 2026-08-29 — INT-224 done: store compliance, and the label that would have lied
- The finding, and it is the whole issue: **"labels generated from the data inventory" is FALSE if the inventory means the schema.** The invariant-18 annotations cover 111 columns and no audio row — because invariant 3 forbids writing raw audio anywhere — so a Data safety / App Privacy declaration derived from `Column.info` states that this product **does not process voice**, about a product whose central feature is a spoken intake and whose manifest asks for RECORD_AUDIO. False in the one direction a store reviewer notices, and nothing would have flagged it. Three more flows fall in the same hole: HealthKit/Health Connect reads (on-device), document bytes (object store), crash diagnostics (no endpoint).
- Fix: an inventory row is a **shape, not a table**. `store/facts.py` carries the same five fields for the four off-schema flows, each citing a repo file that states them, and the app's own manifest is cross-checked **both ways** — a capability with no row fails, and a row for a capability the manifest dropped fails too. Nine capabilities, all classified.
- **No vendor policy was written from memory.** Apple's App Privacy types, Google's Data safety categories, the Play Health apps policy and the IARC questionnaire all move; the loader takes only a dated verbatim snapshot bound by sha256 to the machine-readable file, and none is committed, so every label path fails closed (**HB-A12**, new). The derivation is complete and exercised end-to-end against fixture taxonomies.
- Invariant 20's gate is now **one pattern set in one place** (`intake_platform.legal.claims`): both suites that predated it — INT-020's over docs/security and INT-240's satisfaction scan — read their groups from it, and a test asserts neither can grow a local copy back; `docs/legal` and `docs/regulatory` are declared trees, so the store artefacts under `docs/legal/store/` are inside the scan by where they live. It caught my own README on the first run, which is the shortest proof the tree is really in scope.
- Device-claim gate: a listing describing automated triage or automated instrument interpretation fails closed rather than waiting for App Review — by then it is published. Phrase-shaped, not token-shaped, **because of Turkish**: the required fixed copy contains "tanı" twice and the permitted safety line contains 112. Denials are exempt in both directions (Turkish negates after, English before) and stop at a contrastive conjunction, so "triyaj yapmaz ama aciliyet düzeyi belirler" still reports the second claim.
- The sharpest red of the issue: run against **files/01 §4's own pitch** — "vizit öncesi **not** ve **triyaj** bayrağı" — the gate reported nothing, because **"not" is Turkish for "note"** and a bare English \bnot\b was in the denial set. The product's own promise line carried the token that exempted the claim beside it. Second time in one issue that a general text rule met Turkish and failed silently in the unsafe direction. Also dropped -sız/-siz from the denial set: it is privative, so "kesintisiz triyaj" is a claim.
- Recorded rather than smoothed: files/01 sells "realtime triage" and a "triage flag" while files/06 §0.3 Stage A runs every commercial tenant with device_functions OFF. **The strategy file's own pitch is copy this gate refuses** — which is exactly why §3b calls a marketing claims-control gate a P1 deliverable.
- Checklist: 15 rows, 5 satisfied, 10 blocked, 0 failing. A row with a check must cite a repo file; a row whose obligation is a vendor's published requirement may cite nothing and must name a HUMAN_BLOCKERS id that exists.
- Refused with named asks: the four store taxonomies (HB-A12); whether ephemeral audio is "collected", the Play Health scoping question, the IARC answers, the KVKK ground for crash diagnostics, and **INTAKE's legal entity — which the repo names nowhere** (HB-L21, new).
- **INT-257 filed**: files/01 §3b calls the marketing claims-control gate a P1 deliverable and no issue existed for it. Backlog 203 → 204.
- `make check` exit 0 (baseline on origin/main also 0). `make security` has no rule yet — INT-019; the gates that exist ran clean on the staged tree.

That row is GPU supply; a CI runner needs CPU, and files/00_STATE.md §2 records the AWS İstanbul Local Zone GA since May 2026 with C7i/M7i/R7i — this shape exactly. Filing the TR-DC runner behind HB-I5 would park an available decision behind a constraint that was never true of it. The open rows are HB-I7 (spend) and HB-L11 (physical residency is not legal residency). runners.yaml, the registry row and RUNNER_POOL_ASK all say so, and a test holds the wording.
- Two more findings worth more than the code. (a) A corpus declared synthetic by pattern-scan alone proves only that we can author clean fixtures — files/10 §3 makes the same point about the fixture-only scan — so each corpus is pinned by sha256 and a new identifier can only arrive through a reviewed diff. The two vendored hashes match ATTRIBUTION.md independently. (b) ONE INTEGER IN A HUNDRED PASSES THE TC KIMLIK CHECKSUM, and `GITHUB_RUN_ID` is an eleven-digit integer that changes every run: scanning the runner's own environment naively would have failed ~1 % of CI runs on a value that is not a person. Caught by a test pinning a realistic GitHub environment, not in production. The narrowing is bare integers in GitHub's reserved namespace only — a namespace a workflow cannot write to — and author-chosen content in those variables is still scanned.

Exactly one of the six surfaces overlaps. On it, a path prefix blesses a directory wholesale — a new file dropped into `tests/fixtures/deid/` inherits the exemption silently — where a sha256 declaration cannot. `phi_scan.sh` also covers `docs/`, which this does not. The recommendation in the evidence is: keep both scopes, one mechanism, decided at merge rather than by accident. Concretely handled: this evidence file originally quoted the checksum-valid run id and would have failed INT-019 on merge day; the literal is gone. Flagged separately — `docs/PROGRESS.md` line 464 already carries a TR-mobile-shaped string ON MAIN (INT-145's entry), so INT-019 needs a row or an edit there.
