### 2026-09-03T08:05:00+03:00 · INT-160 · done
- Goal: acceptance verbatim — *"per-tenant endpoint allow-list; push receipts stored; replay-safe"*. A connector send is a **share of clinical data to another organisation**, so invariants 4, 6, 13, 14, 16 and 19 are on one path at once, and ADR-025 already named the five ways an egress allow-list is bypassed — a connector is the component most likely to reach all five.
- Plan: build the framework and no adapter (INT-161 FHIR/SMART, INT-162 HBYS/HL7v2 are the adapters); make the destination registry a **second list enforced by one engine** rather than a second enforcer; answer the two questions the issue title implies but never asks — what an idempotency key is derived from and who is trusted to honour it, and whether a receipt is written before or after the send; ship the registry empty, because a hospital endpoint, a DPA and a credential path are a human's to supply.
- Tests added (with the near-miss negative for any safety rule): **73** across `tests/connectors/test_framework.py` (47), `tests/connectors/test_credentials_and_tls.py` (26) and a shared `connector_harness.py`; plus one new test in `tests/security/test_egress_allowlist.py`. **Near-miss 1 (the fifth bypass):** the endpoint id is real, the row exists, the host is contracted and approved — and the caller is the **wrong tenant**; without it the registry test passes for a lookup that only checks that the id exists. **Near-miss 2 (idempotency identity):** the same note to the same endpoint is suppressed, and an **edited** note is sent, so the ledger is not simply refusing every second call; the key also differs by endpoint and by tenant. **Near-miss 3 (ambiguity):** the *same* `ReadTimeout` against the *same* destination is `failed_retryable` when the DPA declares the key honoured and `needs_human` when it does not — one input, two outcomes, so the parking is the declaration and not the exception type. **Near-miss 4 (split DNS):** a mixed answer containing one good address fails closed rather than picking it. **Near-miss 5 (receiver text):** a `Location` header that is not id-shaped is dropped while the push still settles `delivered`, so the drop is the shape check and not a delivery failure. **Near-miss 6 (the vendor list):** `CLINICAL_RECORD_PUSH` must be **absent** from `infra/security/egress-allowlist.yaml`, which is a tightening of the "no dead members" test rather than an exemption from it. Red run 3 Sep 06:56 local: rc=2, collection `ImportError` — nothing existed.
- Result (`make check` / `make safety` / `make security` summary): MAKE_CHECK_EXIT and MAKE_SAFETY_EXIT recorded in docs/evidence/INT-160.md, both captured with `make …; echo "rc=$?"` and never piped through `tail`. `mypy --strict` clean over 337 source files (the new package is in the Makefile's mypy list); `tests/connectors` 73 passed at **100 % branch** on all eight modules, gated in `check-py` at `--cov-fail-under=95`. Two environment repairs, neither this change's doing: the INT-015 NER cache (symlinked to the shared `~/.cache/intake/deid-tr-ner-v1`) and missing `node_modules` (`pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`). `make security` is INT-019's target and needs the security compose tier — HB-I2 on this machine; reported, not claimed.
- Invariants touched and still passing: **4** — `ConsentGate` is a constructor argument with no default, so an unconsented share cannot be *expressed*; a refusal records a `refused` receipt and a `connector.push_refused` chain entry, because a guard that ran silently is not evidence it ran; attempt and outcome are two hash-chained entries and the suite verifies the chain. **6** — the **response body is never read** (`client.send(..., stream=True)` then `close()` without consuming), so a FHIR `OperationOutcome` echoing the submitted resource cannot become a log line, a receipt field or an exception; `ClinicalPayload` and `OutboundCall` render as a size and a digest; a `Location` reaches a receipt only if id-shaped; two sweeps assert it over `caplog` and `repr(receipt)`. **13** — three families catalogued *before* they were constructed, labelled only by a registry id and a closed receipt state. **14** — the row holds an OpenBao path, never material; `CredentialMissing` names no path (a path in a log is a map of the secret store); `TlsMaterial` overrides `__repr__` **and** `__str__` together, because overriding only `__repr__` leaves `f"{material}"` printing the key. Fixture credentials are deliberately low-entropy — INT-139 tripped INT-019's gitleaks gate with a token-*shaped* fixture, and the fix for that is a boring fixture, never an allow-list entry. **16** — the `connectors` threat row said *"land with INT-160"* in both Signals and Tests; both are now filled in. **17** — the registry ships empty and `auth_mode` has exactly one member rather than a speculative `smart_backend` for INT-161 to inherit. **19** — no new dependency; `cryptography` in one test is already `intake-platform`'s.
- Decisions / ADRs / `00_STATE.md` rows: **ADR-026** (`docs/adr/ADR-026-connector-destinations-second-list-one-engine.md`). **(a) A second list, one enforcement engine.** ADR-025's rule is *one list, two enforcers*; its danger is two lists that **disagree**. A hospital endpoint cannot live in the vendor file — it would then be reachable by every process that loads that file, for every tenant, which is bypass 5 installed as configuration rather than exploited. So the registry is per-tenant data and `ConnectorDestination.egress_entry()` returns the **same `AllowlistEntry` type** the vendor file validates: one implementation of what an entry means, and the resolve-once-and-pin, denied-range, redirect and `INTAKE_ALLOW_PHI_EGRESS` gates are all INT-018's code. `EgressPurpose` gains one member, `CLINICAL_RECORD_PUSH`, which is then **forbidden** in the vendor file by test. **(b) The idempotency key is derived, and the receiver is not trusted.** `sha256(tenant \x1f endpoint \x1f resource_ref \x1f sha256(payload))` — so a retry is a retry, an edited note is a new share, and a second hospital is a new delivery; consent is deliberately absent from the derivation, because re-granting a withdrawn scope does not make an unchanged record a different record. The key travels on the wire so a receiver **can** deduplicate, and the **local ledger is the authority** because *"we sent it twice and the hospital deduplicated"* is not a promise this system may make on a hospital's behalf. Each row therefore declares `receiver_idempotency` with **no default**: an ambiguous outcome is retryable only where the DPA says the key is honoured, and parks `needs_human` where it does not — an auto-retry into a receiver that may not deduplicate risks a duplicate clinical record, which is worse than a push that visibly did not complete. **(c) The receipt is written BEFORE the send.** There is no third option. Written after, it cannot record a push that left and never came back; written before, it can claim an attempt that never left. Invariant 4 makes the first the worse failure — an unrecorded share is an unauditable disclosure nobody can discover — so write-ahead, with the cost paid in the open: the row says `attempted` and **never** `delivered`, the outcome is a second transition and a second chain entry, and a crash in between leaves an open receipt and an `in_flight` key that nothing resolves by guessing (asserted by driving `KeyboardInterrupt` out of the transport). Same shape INT-137 took refusing to mark a DSAR `COMPLETED` at the moment of delivery. **(d) The sender is synchronous** — the egress guard is a synchronous `httpx` transport, and an async connector would need a second implementation of resolve-once-and-pin, which is the two-enforcers-that-disagree failure ADR-025 exists to prevent.
- Blocked on (human + one sentence + HUMAN_BLOCKERS id): **nothing blocks the acceptance**, which is met as a mechanism with the content empty. What does not exist is any **destination**, and it is not an agent's to invent: a row costs five recorded human facts — `dpa_ref` (an executed DPA naming this interface), `approval_ref` (the tier-3 approval), `credential_ref` and `client_cert_ref` (OpenBao paths that already hold material), and `receiver_idempotency` (what the receiver's agreement says). Filed in docs/backlog/blockers.md. **HB-A5** covers the *adapter* half — what interfaces Acıbadem exposes — and no HB row covers the destination half; **no blocker id was minted here**, because ids move under concurrent merges and predicting one has collided every time. Coordinator to allocate. **ADR-026 was also taken un-coordinated**: 026 was free on `origin/main` at branch time and another agent could claim it concurrently.
- Next: INT-161 (FHIR push adapter) and INT-162 (HBYS/SBYS kit) both depend on this framework and both carry `approval:human-vendor`; INT-163 (signed QR check-in) depends on it and on INT-143.

The suppression pins are on **entries, not raw lines** — a raw line count punishes the sentence explaining a row and waves through the terse mute, which is backwards. Two further pins guard acceptance line 2 structurally rather than by narrative: a floor of 88 vitest files, and a ban on the rest of vitest's skip vocabulary (`describe.skip`, the whole `.only` family, `skipIf`/`runIf`, `xit`/`xdescribe`) that `no_placeholders.sh` does not reach.
- Result (`make check` / `make safety` / `make security` summary): **CHECK_RC=0, SAFETY_RC=0, SECURITY_RC=0**, each captured with `make …; echo "rc=$?"` and never piped through `tail`. check-py 5476 passed at 95.44 %, mypy --strict clean; triage 530 at 100 % branch with 18/18 mutations detected; voice-runtime 1271 at 98.55 %; check-ts **86 files / 1753 tests** mobile and **2 / 6** schemas — identical to the pre-bump baseline, not merely "no worse"; check-i18n 30. `osv-scanner scan source -r .` goes from *"Total 6 packages affected by 8 known vulnerabilities (1 Critical, 3 High, 4 Medium)"*, rc 1, to **`No issues found`, rc 0**. `make security` **does not exist on `main`** — it is INT-019's target and PR #233 is still open — so it was measured on a throwaway worktree holding `int-19-secgates2` merged with this branch: **[6/12] before, all twelve green after**, with step 12's own `osv-scanner (known-vulnerable pin)` control still rejecting a planted case, so step 6 passing is a clean tree and not a broken scanner.
- Invariants touched and still passing: **17** — this issue is *specifically* about not silencing things, and nothing was silenced: no suppression file exists on this branch, none was created, no test was deleted, skipped or narrowed, and both `vitest.config.ts` files are byte-identical to before. **8** — the coverage floors did not move to accommodate the bump; `packages/clinical-schemas` still reports 100 % against its unchanged floor of 85, and every python lane cleared its own floor. **19** — fifteen packages verified MIT from the raw LICENSE blob as installed, grant text present, never from a badge or a `license:` field; INT-019's own `check_licenses.py` accepts the bumped tree with **no allow-list change**. **14** — the gate set is unweakened and now runs end to end.
- Decisions / ADRs / `00_STATE.md` rows: no ADR — this is a dependency bump, not architecture. **(a) `vitest` is a declaration and the other four are overrides.** `vitest` is the only one of the six this repo names; `vite`/`esbuild` arrive under it and `uuid`/`decode-uri-component` under `@expo/ngrok`, `xcode` and `expo-router → query-string`. Declaring a transitive as a direct dependency to move it would put `vite` in `devDependencies` as something this repo never imports; an override says what it actually is — a version floor imposed on somebody else's tree. **(b) Every override carries a version selector**, `"uuid@<11.1.1"` not `"uuid"`. The uuid advisory has three disjoint affected ranges (`0→11.1.1`, `12.0.0→12.0.1`, `13.0.0→13.0.1`); a blanket pin would silently drag a future dependency that legitimately wanted uuid 14 backwards into a version chosen once for reasons that had nothing to do with it. **(c) The bump stops at vitest 3 / vite 6 although the registry carries vitest 5 and vite 8.** Those two are where the advisories are fixed and what the acceptance names; three further majors is a different change with a different blast radius, and `vitest@3` declares `vite: ^5 || ^6` anyway. **(d) A resolution surprise worth the next person's hour:** bumping vitest alone did *not* move vite — pnpm keeps an existing resolution while the new range still admits it, so `vite@5.4.21` and `esbuild@0.21.5` survived `vitest 2 → 3` and four of the eight advisories stayed live. The `vite@<6.4.3` override is what actually moves them.
- Blocked on (human + one sentence + HUMAN_BLOCKERS id): **nothing blocks the acceptance, and no id was minted — the coordinator allocates.** Three things reported rather than fixed. (1) **Three `@esbuild/*` platform binaries cannot be licence-verified from the artifact**: `netbsd-arm64`, `openbsd-arm64` and `openharmony-arm64` are new names in the lockfile and their tarballs contain three files with **no LICENSE at all**, so the only statement inside them is a `package.json` field, which is a card tag. Verified instead from the raw `LICENSE.md` of the esbuild source at tag `v0.25.12` (MIT, full grant text) and confirmed the omission is pre-existing — `@esbuild/netbsd-x64@0.21.5`, already in the tree, has the identical layout. Recorded as *verified at the source tag, not in the artifact*, which is deliberately a weaker claim than the other fifteen. (2) **INT-310's `tests:` field and INT-019's name the same file**, so merging the two produces exactly one conflict, an add/add on `tests/ci/test_security_gates.py`; it resolves by concatenation and the merged file passes — done and verified in the probe worktree so the recipe is on record rather than discovered under pressure. (3) **`vitest` is declared in two manifests** (root and `apps/mobile`) with no `catalog:` entry binding them, so the next bump has two places to miss; making that one declaration is a workspace-layout change with its own blast radius and is not smuggled in here.

- Plan: measure both candidate encodings **before** writing anything, because the clause asks for a decision and a decision needs numbers. Then one codec at one place rather than three repairs: `encode_json_value`/`decode_json_value` on the `JsonValue` alias, shared by the overlay envelope so there is a single definition of how a quantity crosses JSON, and deliberately **not** shared by the FHIR mappers.

- Decisions / ADRs / `00_STATE.md` rows: **ADR-029** (id allocated by the coordinator — ADR-028 is taken by the unmerged `int-203-edge`, which is why `main` looks like it stops at 027). *A clinical quantity crosses INTAKE's own wire as a tagged JSON string.* **(a) Neither obvious encoding was available, and both were measured first**: a JSON number cannot carry a declared scale at all (`TypeAdapter(Decimal).validate_json("38.50")` → `Decimal("38.5")`), and a bare JSON string cannot be told apart from a string answer, which this grammar legitimately carries. **(b) The ambiguity was already committed**: the exported `JsonScalar` carried two indistinguishable `string` members, so no consumer could decide between them — this repairs a defect rather than choosing an encoding for a green field, and the regenerated schema now spells three mutually exclusive string members. **(c) The rejected alternative is recorded**: an object wrapper `{"__decimal__": "38.50"}` needs no escape scheme and therefore imposes no rule every plain string pays forever, but it turns a *scalar* position into an object — scalar arrays become arrays of objects — and it relocates the ambiguity onto a reserved dict key rather than removing it. **(d) The FHIR carve-out is deliberate and sits in Consequences**: R4 requires `decimal` to be a JSON number, so `QrAnswer.value_decimal` widened to `Decimal | float | None` and still serialises to a number; trailing zeros normalise there, which is a foreign format's constraint and not ours. **(e) The decode runs in python mode as well as JSON mode, and that is the decision's stated cost**: FastAPI validates an already-parsed body in python mode, so a json-only decoder would leave every inbound API quantity a string — the bug itself, at the front door. The price is that a model constructed in Python takes wire form, pinned by name in the ADR against the test that asserts it (`d1f3bf1`).

### 2026-09-04T11:30:00Z · INT-306 · done
- Goal: the schema records a visit DATE and the expiry rule needs a visit DATETIME. Acceptance verbatim: *"the slot carries a time of day where one is known, and states that it is unknown where it is not — an absent time must stay absent rather than defaulting to midnight, because a default midnight is indistinguishable from a real 00:00 appointment and silently shortens every link by up to a day"*; *"every producer of a visit time is enumerated and each says which it is: patient entry, an MHRS/e-Nabız connector field, or a document extraction — and an extracted time carries the same provenance an extracted value carries elsewhere, never arriving unattributed"*; *"INT-150's approximation is replaced rather than duplicated: link_expires_at takes the datetime where one exists and keeps the start-of-day anchor only as the documented fallback, with a test asserting the fallback is never LATER than the true appointment"*.
- Plan: add the field the two issues that filed this were each missing from opposite ends — INT-150 could not compute `visit_datetime + 72 h` and approximated it with the start of the visit day; INT-122 parsed `14:30` off an MHRS message and `as_visit_fields()` dropped it because the API had no field for it. Put the vocabulary in `clinical-schemas` so the wire boundary and the extraction path cannot disagree about the spelling or the provenance; make `link_expires_at` take a `VisitSchedule` so the approximation is *replaced* rather than defaulted around; store the hour as one more row in the already-inventoried `slots` table, INT-134's precedent, so there is no new table, no new column and no new inventory annotation.
- Tests added (with the near-miss negative for any safety rule): 26 in `tests/schemas/test_visit_time_vocabulary.py` (the type-level half — the states that are unconstructible rather than merely rejected), 17 in `tests/api/test_visit_time.py` (the field travelling: body → `visit.time` slot → read-back → INT-122's extraction end to end), 5 new in `tests/clinician/test_doctor_link.py` plus 2 of INT-150's renamed to say which of the two anchor rules they pin. **The near-misses.** *(a)* `test_a_real_midnight_is_not_the_absent_time` — a `00:00` booking and an unknown hour produce the same anchor instant (necessarily; they are the same instant) and must not produce the same record; an implementation defaulting to `time.min` passes every expiry assertion in this repository and fails only here. *(b)* Provenance in **both** directions, so neither "always require a ref" nor "never require one" survives: an extracted or connector time with no `source_ref` (and with a whitespace-only one) raises, and a `source_ref` attached to a time the patient typed raises too. *(c)* `test_the_fallback_is_never_later_than_the_true_appointment` — the clause's own property over 144 minutes spanning the whole day, asserting `fallback <= exact` **and** that equality holds at exactly one minute; without the second half an implementation that ignored the recorded time would pass. *(d)* `test_a_time_that_is_not_hh_mm_is_refused` — `9:30`, `14:30:00`, `14:30:00.5`, `24:00`, `14:60`, `2:30 PM`, `14.30`, `1430`, `""`; `time.fromisoformat` accepts two of those for the same minute, which is exactly why it is not what is used. *(e)* The controls that must still be ACCEPTED: a dated visit with no hour is still created and still gets a link on the start-of-day anchor, and an undated visit is still refused (`visit_date_unknown`) — a date is the whole anchor, an hour only sharpens it. Green 194 passed, GREEN_EXIT=0.

What makes the claim checkable rather than free is that the two non-patient sources must name the artefact, and that `time_source` is validated against the enum rather than stored as whatever string arrived. **(c) Provenance is DERIVED in `as_visit_fields()`**, from the candidate's own `document_ref`, for the same reason `confirm_visit_candidate` takes no `by=`: a caller who could pass provenance in could pass a different one. **(d) The read path re-parses the slot through the same validator** rather than constructing a `VisitTime` directly, so a row that could not have been created through the API cannot be read out of the database either — and a malformed row raises instead of degrading to "no time recorded", because that degrade is indistinguishable from an honest unknown. **(e) An empty `time` field is `bad_time`, not "absent"**: a client that sends the field is asserting it has a value.

- Plan: separate the two halves the spec sentence had welded together, because conflating them is how it came to read as coverage. **Geo** is already enforced and by nothing this issue writes — `normalise_tr_phone` admits a Turkish mobile and nothing else, so the international premium ranges pumping profits from never reach a delivery adapter; that gets a test, not a claim. **Carrier** is a new module with an authority seam and a committed table that ships with zero rows, because which `5XX` prefixes the BTK has allocated is a published fact nobody here has read from the publication (HUMAN_BLOCKERS rule 1), and it fails in the expensive direction: a prefix wrongly recorded as unallocated refuses every real subscriber behind it.

Plus: the shipped table is pinned empty and unable to refuse, a prefix list with no `source` block is refused by the model, `exhaustive` has no default, a refusal costs the caller its budget (proving the check sits *after* the abuse gate), the Redis key grammar and the code record's field set are unchanged, and a refusal writes no audit row.
